# Positions and approach roads, scope B (Union II, III and XII Corps): summary

Scope B of the positions ledger (brief: `GOVERNANCE/briefs/positions.md`). Claim records: `positions_B.jsonl` in this folder (built by `tools/bt/positions_claims.py` from `tools/bt/positions_data_B_*.py`; this summary is generated by `tools/bt/positions_summary.py` and then carries the helpers' notes verbatim). Nothing in this folder changes a default, gate or tier elsewhere. Every conflict is carried with both values and never resolved; the owner decides defaults (CLAUDE.md, Law 6).

## How the claims were made

- Text: OR Ser. I vol. 27, Part 1 (Union reports), Part 3 (correspondence and orders) and, where a Part 2 report speaks of a unit in scope, Part 2; index `library/or27_index.json`; single-witness OCR (no second text yet: OR27-NOTES.md "What remains" item 1). Page numbers come from the index; a `~` marks an interpolated page range and is never cited as exact.
- Every quote (<15 words) is located and re-verified by code at build time; every time phrase, place phrase and road phrase is verbatim within the stated window of the quote (700 characters by default; claims that needed a wider window, such as a dispatch whose date line is far from the sentence, record that in the code, not in the data). Every claim carries its own verified extract (<15 words; the matched sentence fragment of that claim, trimmed by code to 14 words around its time/place phrases when longer); claims taken from one sentence may share the same extract. Clock times damaged by OCR have no `t_iso` (`when.description` says "OCR glyph only"); see `positions_B_overrides.json`.
- Tiers: `documented` where an OR passage states exactly the claim; `inferred` with `reasoning` where it does not (very few: grep `"tier": "inferred"`); `open gap` where no OR passage was found, with the searches tried. Orders (Part 3) are recorded as orders, not as reports of execution; the note on the claim says so.
- "Brigade" claims often rest on the brigade commander's report and on regimental reports; regiments, battalions and batteries that the OR names with their own position or movement carry their own `unit_id` from `history/units.json`.
- The Part 2 (Confederate) reports were searched by unit and commander names; they name few of the Union units in scope (hits for the Second and Twelfth Corps commanders are isolated; Third Corps hits are in the Confederate corps and division reports) and were used only where the helper notes say so.

## Second Corps: approach roads and times found (what decides where units enter the map)

All of this is from the claim file; ids in brackets. The Second Corps reached Gettysburg on the Taneytown road; the OR names the road at brigade level only for the last stretch.

- **June 28:** corps marched from near Sugar Loaf Mountain and was stopped by Meade near Monocacy Junction. General Orders put the Second Corps on the picket front "From Monocacy Junction to the bridge above Carroll Creek" for the night (Part 3 D0839).
- **June 29:** army order D0840: Second Corps to march at 4 a. m. "by Johnsville, Liberty, and Union, to Frizellburg". Hancock: the order was delayed and the corps was "not in motion until 8 a. m."; halted at 10 p. m. one mile beyond Uniontown after 30 miles. Brigades: Brooke left the Monocacy bridge about 6 a. m., 2nd DE at 6 a. m., 53rd PA at 8 a. m., Webb at 6 a. m. (9 p. m. at Uniontown), Hays's division at 1 p. m. (3 a. m. June 30 at Uniontown). Order and execution differ (conflict group on the 4 a. m. / 8 a. m. / 6 a. m. times).
- **June 30:** corps in camp at Uniontown (Hancock; Part 3 D0937 shows no movement). Part 3 D0932 (1 p. m.) orders it to Taneytown and D0919 lists "Second Corps to Taneytown" as the destination of the movement of July 1.
- **July 1:** Brooke's brigade left Uniontown at 6 a. m. (Brooke) or 8 a. m. (Baily), Smyth at 6.30 a. m. (Hays's division, Smyth), Hancock's corps at Taneytown about 11 a. m., corps departure "toward Gettysburg at 1.30 p. m." (OCR "1..30"). Hazard's artillery brigade left Taneytown at 2 p. m. and went into position "3 miles southeast of Gettysburg, on the Taneytown road" at 11 p. m. (claim on us-2c-art, kind arrival_road). Night camps by distance from Gettysburg: Webb about 4 miles; Brooke about 3 miles (arrival about 9.30 p. m.); 2nd DE 5 or 6 miles (about 9 p. m.); Kelly 2 or 3 miles (about 10 p. m.); Hays's division about 3 miles (right of the Taneytown road, 1st DE); 4th Ohio 1 1/2 miles in rear of the cemetery; Hancock "about 3 miles in rear of the town"; 7th Mich 3 or 4 miles south; Devereux (19th Mass) about 2 miles. Two Part 3 documents of July 1 place the corps differently ("now at Taneytown ... held in reserve"; "on the road between Taneytown and Gettysburg").
- **July 2:** march resumed at 3 a. m. (Brooke; the 111th NY, Seeley), 4 a. m. (Smyth places his brigade on the hills; 1st DE arrives about 4 a. m.), 4.10 a. m. (64th NY, which crosses the Taneytown road at 6.10 a. m. and forms at 7 a. m. "a half mile southwest of Cemetery Hill"), 4.30 a. m. (Kelly), "at daylight" (Hancock, Hazard), 5 a. m. (59th NY "on the road leading to Gettysburg"; 7th Mich "on the Gettysburg road"). Arrival: Part 3 D2099 (6.15 a. m.) "The Second Corps is up"; Hancock "about 7 a. m."; brigade times 6 a. m. (145th PA, 2nd DE), 6.30 a. m. (Webb on "Granite Ridge"), 7 a. m., 8 a. m. (53rd PA, Carroll's brigade formed "between Woodruff's battery on the left and the Taney town road on the right"), about 10 a. m. (52nd NY, 69th NY). Position on arrival: Third Division (Hays) on the right "near the cemetery", Second (Gibbon) in the centre, First (Caldwell) on the left; Hays: "moving on the Taneytown road, arrived within about a mile of the town ... a ridge nearly parallel with the road, facing westward".
- **Departure (July 4-5):** army circular D1099 sends "The Twelfth and Second Corps, via Taneytown, Middleburg, and Woodsborough, through Frederick to Middletown". Brigade departures from the field on July 5 at noon (Webb, 53rd PA), about 3 p. m. (2nd DE), 4 p. m. (Brooke, 64th NY, 145th PA), 4.30 p. m. (Kelly), 5 p. m. (Oliver, 145th PA), to Two Taverns by the Baltimore pike (53rd PA, 145th PA name it); 116th PA (Mulholland) has the march on the afternoon of July 4. Not resolved.

## Second Corps: differences from `history/arrivals.json` and `app/data/placement.json` (reported, not applied)

1. Gate us-2c (first on field): OR gives 06:15 a. m. "up" (Part 3), about 7 a. m. (Hancock), and brigade times from 3 a. m. to 10 a. m.; the ledger values are 22:30 Jul 1, 06:00 and 07:00. The OR has Smyth's brigade "placed in position on the hills overlooking the town" at 4 a. m. and Dent's 1st Delaware arriving "about 4 a. m." (Third Division, gate floor 00:00 Jul 2: no contradiction); Gate us-2c-2d (06:00, EPP): OR 2nd DE in line "about 6 a. m.", 145th PA 6 a. m., Webb 6.30 a. m., 64th NY 7 a. m. (not before 06:00). No OR passage supports 22:30 Jul 1 on the field; the corps night bivouac was 2-6 miles out until about 3-4.30 a. m. Q04 is not contradicted; the 4 a. m. times are listed so that the owner sees them.
2. Ledger night position "Jun 30: Taneytown": the OR (Hancock R076 p.367; Webb; Hays) has the corps in camp at Uniontown on June 30 and at Taneytown only on July 1 about 11 a. m. "Jun 29: gap": the OR gives Uniontown (halt 1 mile beyond, 10 p. m.).
3. placement.json PO-22 (71st PA "sent to Culp's Hill" at night): the OR sends the 71st PA with the 106th PA to the Eleventh Corps front near the cemetery (Hancock R076 p.372; Webb R104; Smith R106), not to Slocum's front; no OR passage puts the 71st PA at Culp's Hill.
4. PO-21 (Carroll to East Cemetery Hill about 20:00): OR "about dark" (Carroll), "nearly dark" (Hancock), 7.30 p. m. (4th Ohio), "about 8 o'clock" (7th WV), 9.30 p. m. (Howard: old position resumed, Carroll between Ames and Wadsworth).
5. PO-15/PO-18/PO-19 (Caldwell to the Wheatfield 16:00-18:00, engaged 17:30-18:00): consistent with the OR as a range (departure times 4 p. m. McKeen, 4-6 Caldwell, about 5 Kelly/Brooke/Stroh, about 6 Broady; engaged about 5.30 Byrnes).
6. PO-05/AC-03/PO-26 (III Corps), PO-36..PO-41 (XII Corps): see the helper notes below.

## Second Corps: index gaps and OCR problems

- No own report in the index for: Sickles (III Corps); the five battery commanders of the Second Corps artillery brigade (Woodruff, Arnold, Cushing, Brown, Rorty); 27th CT (Merwin killed), 148th PA, 108th NY, 126th NY, 1st Company Massachusetts Sharpshooters (the last appears only in the organization table R009). Lieut. Col. Pierce's 108th NY and Colonel Sherrill's 126th NY are placed by Smyth R123 and Bull R128. Reports are placed through neighbours as the claims show.
- OCR damage of note: "Taneyiown", "Taneji;own" (Taneytown); "1..30 p. m." (Hancock p.369); "13 m." for noon (Smyth, Part 1 p.464); "13 o'clock" (Webb); "June 39" and "Jiily"; the date slips in the print itself (53rd PA "July 3" for July 2; 116th PA "morning of the 3d"; Webb's "assault of the 3d at about 6.30 p. m." in the July 2 passage; 42nd NY "morning of the 3d"). Each is flagged in the claim note. Tables (casualty returns, organization) are numerically unreadable and are not used. The Part 3 position report of the Second Corps (D2103) has a scrambled diagram.

## Third and Twelfth Corps: headline findings (from the helper notes appended below; full detail and claim ids there)

- **III Corps (641 claims).** No report by Sickles or Warren is in the index. First Division: Emmitsburg to Gettysburg on July 1 (Birney 5.30 p. m.; other reports about dark to 7 p. m.; ledger gate 20:00); de Trobriand follows on July 2 up the pike from Emmitsburg and is with the division about 9-10 a. m. Second Division: Waynesborough pike, Marsh Run road, Black Horse Tavern road and a counter-march; bivouac 1.00-2.30 a. m. July 2 (Brewster "about midnight"); no OR passage gives the ledger's 21:00 for the counter-march. Artillery: Taneytown road on July 1. Conflicts include the 5 a. m. Geary relief against Birney's 7 a. m., advance times from 1-2 p. m. to 4.30 p. m., the July 3 move to the centre from 10 a. m. to 5 p. m., and the July 6-7 departures. No own reports for the 8th NJ, 115th PA, 12th NH, 73rd NY, 74th NY (gap claims written).
- **XII Corps (794 claims).** R277 (3rd Maryland) is upside down and unreadable; R273 and R292 have inferred start pages; enclosures inside R271, R272 and R006 are unindexed. Corps, June 30: Littlestown, road to Hanover. July 1: Two Taverns, then the Baltimore pike (Williams) or the turnpike from Littlestown to Gettysburg (Ruger). First Division left the pike about 2 miles out and took a cross-road to the Hanover road; regiments arrived 12 m. to 4 p. m. Second Division: Two Taverns 11 a. m., advanced about 3 p. m. on the Baltimore turnpike, line on the extreme left 5 p. m. Lockwood: from Baltimore via Frederick, at Gettysburg about 8 a. m. July 2 (no road or hour between Monocacy and Gettysburg). About 30 conflict groups (e.g. the July 2 departure from the works 4 to 7.30 p. m.; the date digits in the Williams/Lockwood reports; Geary's route). Ledger differences: no OR source for "Ruger at Spangler's Spring 23:00"; Spangler's Spring, Powers' Hill and Wolf Hill are never named in the OR; "Cemetery Ridge 17:30" for Geary and the July 1 Culp's Hill works of the 123rd NY are not supported as stated; gate Q04 for us-12c (17:30) is later than OR hours given for some units (reported, not applied).

The verdict column in the generated table below is a mechanical rule applied to all three corps; the helper notes carry their own, stricter, per-brigade judgements (they agree that Lockwood and the artillery brigades are the weakest).

### Per brigade, division and artillery brigade: coverage

"Own" = claims on the unit itself plus its regiments, battalions and batteries. "Road of approach (own)" = an own claim up to July 2 whose place carries a road phrase (count). "Position on field" = own position/engagement/movement/night_position claims on July 2-3 with a printed place (count). Verdict rule: **full** = own OR claims for approach (June 28 - July 2 march, bivouac or arrival), a July 2 field position/action, a July 3 position/action and a departure on July 4 or later; **partial** = some but not all (what is missing is named); **none** = no OR passage found. This describes the OR record held in the claim file, not a historical judgement.

### Open-gap claims (NOT_FOUND recorded with the searches tried): 25

- **POS-B-us-3c-81** (III Corps, kind strength_note): Sickles filed no report in OR vol. 27 (his testimony is in the Committee on the Conduct of the War). The index has no record under Sickles; casualty returns are tables that the OCR cannot read. Strength figures per division/brigade are taken from the brigade and regimental reports instead.
  - searches: Third Corps total effective strength July 1 | Third Corps present for duty June 30 | Sickles report Gettysburg Third Corps
  - reports searched: OR27-1-R133, OR27-1-R134, OR27-1-R181, OR27-1-R006, OR27-1-R013
- **POS-B-us-3c-1d-graham-18** (Graham, kind arrival_road): None of the six regimental reports of Graham's brigade (Nelson R136, Danks R137, Tippin R138, Craig R139, Bowen R140, Madill R141) names the road used on the march from Emmitsburg to Gettysburg on July 1; Graham himself filed no report (wounded and captured). Craig names the Hagerstown road at Emmitsburg; Danks names the "Emmitsburg pike" only for the picket line of July 1-2.
  - searches: Graham First Brigade road Gettysburg July 1 evening | Emmitsburg road Fifty-seventh Sixty-third Sixty-eighth Pennsylvania July 1 | pike Gettysburg One hundred and fifth One hundred and forty-first Pennsylvania
  - reports searched: OR27-1-R136, OR27-1-R137, OR27-1-R138, OR27-1-R139, OR27-1-R140, OR27-1-R141, OR27-1-R133
- **POS-B-us-3c-1d-ward-11** (Ward, kind arrival_road): Ward (R135) says "a forced march over horrible roads" without naming the road; Taylor, Lakeman, Higgins, Cummins and Sawyer (4th Me) do not name it either. Only Moore R148 (99th Pa) says "by way of the Emmitsburg road".

Langley) is indexed in OR vol. 27; Carr R160 and Humphreys R158 name it only in the order of battle, and Carr's own narrative names the 1st, 11th, 16th Mass, 11th NJ, 26th Pa (and the 84th Pa as absent). Its place on the field and its dates are open.

- **POS-B-us-3c-2d-27** (2nd Div Humphreys, kind movement): The ledger (arrivals.json us-3c-2d, AP-07) prints a counter-march at about 21:00 on the Black Horse Tavern road. Neither Humphreys R158, Carr R160 nor any regiment of the division gives a clock time for reaching the Black Horse Tavern road or for turning back; the only hours are the 3 p. m. order to march, 1 / 1.30 / 2 / 2.30 a. m. for the bivouac at Gettysburg, and Brewster's "about midnight".

### Approach claims that decide where units enter the map (June 28 - July 2)

Claims of kind `arrival_road`, plus any claim up to July 2 whose place carries a road phrase. "t" is the literal clock conversion when a clock time is printed; citation is report id and printed page (~ = interpolated page range).

- POS-B-us-3c-24: Time and date are an editorial bracket in the printed text ("5.25 [p. M., July 1, 1863,]"). Birney R133 says his leading brigades reached Gettysburg at 5.30 p. m.
- POS-B-us-3c-48: Place OCR "Piizer's". Addressed to Birney commanding Third Corps (received 1.30 p. m.).
- POS-B-us-3c-50: Meade to the Sixth Corps: General Birney, Third Corps, is placed in the Sixth Corps column in the order of march
- POS-B-us-3c-54: OCR date "June 38". Randolph R181 says the corps marched that afternoon to "Woodville" (not resolved: Woodsborough vs Woodville vs Walkersville).
- POS-B-us-3c-55: The date line before this sentence is OCR-damaged; sequence puts it on June 29 (the next sentence group is headed June 30).
- POS-B-us-3c-58: OCR "Jidy 7".

- POS-B-us-3c-72: OCR "end" for and.

## Helper notes: notes_B_III.md

# Scope B, III Corps: notes for the positions summary

Built with `python -I tools/bt/positions_claims.py B III` from `tools/bt/positions_data_B_III_a.py` .. `_f.py` into `docs/battle-theatre/history/positions/partial_B_III.jsonl`. Every quote, time phrase, place phrase and road phrase is located by code in the named OR record. Nothing here changes a default, gate or tier elsewhere. All conflicts below are carried unresolved (both values kept, linked through `conflicts_with`).

Module map: `_a` corps (us-3c) and Part 3 dispatches; `_b` First Division level, Ward's brigade, its regiments and the two sharpshooter regiments; `_c` Graham's and de Trobriand's brigades and regiments; `_d` Second Division, Carr's brigade and regiments; `_e` Brewster's and Burling's brigades and regiments; `_f` artillery brigade and batteries.

## 2. Coverage verdicts (units with their own claim set)

Verdict scale: full = hours, place and road of approach documented from June 30/July 1 to July 5 or later; partial = positions and hours documented but a named element missing (road of approach, a day, the commander's own report); none = no claim.

| Unit | verdict | road of approach documented | position on the field documented | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Corps (Sickles) | full for the marches and July 1-7 dispatches; no report by Sickles | yes: Taneytown-Bridgeport-Emmitsburg-Gettysburg; "Emmitsburg road" (Hancock R076 p369); two parallel roads (D0973) | yes, July 2 (Emmitsburg road, Peach Orchard) | Sickles filed no report in vol. 27 (see section 6); evidence is Birney, Humphreys, Randolph, Hunt, Hancock, Meade, Gibbon, Part 3 |
| 1st Division (Birney) | partial | road not named by Birney; "Emmitsburg road" only through the 99th Pa (R148) and Hancock for the corps | yes (7 a. m. line, Peach Orchard, Sugar Loaf) | arrival hour conflicts (5.30 p. m. to about 7 p. m.), no named road in the division report |
| Graham | partial | no: none of the six regimental reports names the road (gap claim) | yes, with hours | Graham wounded and captured, no report; position in Peach Orchard / brick house / Emmitsburg road fully documented from Birney, Tippin, Craig, Bowen, Nelson, Madill |
| Ward | partial | only the 99th Pa names it (Emmitsburg road) (gap claim) | yes: rocky eminence near Round Top, stone wall, wheat-field, with hours | Ward's own report has times but no road |
| de Trobriand | full | yes: "pike leading to Gettysburg" (Egan R156, Merrill R153), Taneytown road (Pierce R154) | yes (apex, wheat-field, Peach Orchard skirmish line) | all five regiments report; start hour differs (2 a. m. order, 3, 4.30, daybreak) |
| 2nd Division (Humphreys) | full | yes: Waynesborough pike, western road nearly parallel to the main road, Marsh Run crossing, Black Horse Tavern road, counter-march, main road to Gettysburg | yes (Emmitsburg road line, log house, Round Top ridge) | only the clock time of the counter-march is missing (gap claim) |
| Carr | full | yes (left-hand road past Marsh Creek, Black Horse Tavern road, main road) | yes (left center; old building in orchard on left of line) | report plus six regiments; 84th Pa was with the train, 12th NH no report |
| Brewster (Excelsior) | partial | no (gap claim); division route from Humphreys | yes (rear of Carr, 71st/72nd left, 74th right, 73rd log house, then Graham) | hours differ for the night arrival |
| Burling | partial | only via Humphreys (main road from Emmitsburg) (gap claim) | yes (massed, then split among Graham, Ward, picket; each regiment placed) | the brigade was dispersed; 8th NJ and 115th Pa have no own report |
| Artillery brigade (Randolph) | partial | yes to Gettysburg July 1 (near the Taneytown road, R181) and on July 2 | yes for all five batteries, with hours | nothing in the OR for July 3-7 as a unit (gap claim); Randolph wounded, Clark acting chief |

Brigades with NO claim: none. Units with an open gap: us-3c (Sickles' own report), us-3c-1d-graham and -ward (road of approach), us-3c-2d (hour of the Black Horse Tavern counter-march), us-3c-2d-brewster and -burling (road of approach), us-3c-art (July 3-7), 1st RI E (July 3 onward), 8th NJ, 115th Pa, 12th NH, 73rd NY, 74th NY (no regimental report indexed). Reports searched for each gap are listed inside the gap records (`reports_searched`, `searches_tried`).

Brigade-level verdict count (six brigades + artillery brigade): full 2 (de Trobriand, Carr), partial 5 (Graham, Ward, Brewster, Burling, Artillery), none 0. Divisions: 2nd full, 1st partial. Corps: full for movements, no report by its commander.

## 3. Approach roads and hours found (these decide where units enter the map)

First Division (Birney)
- June 28: Middletown to Frederick (Sickles resumed the corps there), then Walkersville "on the road to Taneytown" (R133 p482; itinerary R007 p144 says near Woodsborough; Randolph R181 p581 says Woodville).
- June 29: Taneytown (Randolph R181; itinerary R007). Birney does not mention it.
- June 30: bivouac until 3 p. m., ordered to Emmitsburg (R133); bivouac within about 1.5 miles of Emmitsburg (R133, OCR "1^"); Sickles at Bridgeport 7.45 p. m. says the First Division and two batteries are farther toward Emmitsburg, across Middle Creek (D0936 p425), about a mile this side of Emmitsburg (D0935 p424); 105th Pa encamped about a mile east of Emmitsburg (R139 p500).
- July 1: took position beyond Emmitsburg covering the Fairfield and Gettysburg road; ordered to Gettysburg "at 2 o'clock" (R133) or 3.30 p. m. (D0974); left Emmitsburg 1.15 p. m. (105th), about 2 p. m. (57th, 114th); reached Gettysburg 5.30 p. m. (Birney), about dark (Ward), about 7 p. m. (114th), a short time after sunset by the Emmitsburg road (99th, R148 p513); bivouac south of the town / 1 mile south (114th R140, 105th R139); batteries near the Taneytown road (R181).
- De Trobriand (3rd Brigade) and Winslow's battery stayed at Emmitsburg July 1; order to rejoin at 2 a. m. (R152) or 1.30 a. m. (Burling for his own brigade); left at 3 a. m. (3rd Mich), daybreak (R152), 4.30 a. m. (17th Me); "up the pike leading to Gettysburg" (40th NY R156 p526), formed near the Taneytown road (3rd Mich R154 p523); with the division about 9 a. m. (Birney), about 10 (de Trobriand, Merrill), about 12 m. (Pierce).

Second Division (Humphreys)
- June 28: through Frederick, bivouac 7 miles out on the Woodsborough road (R158 p530); June 29 Taneytown through Woodsborough and Bruceville (R158); Carr left 5.30 a. m. (R160 p542) and arrived 4 p. m. (Baldwin R161), 6 p. m. (Tripp R162), about 5 p. m. (Donovan R163).
- June 30: left Taneytown 2 p. m. (26th Pa), 3 p. m. (1st and 11th Mass), 4.30 p. m. (Carr, R160), "morning" (72nd NY R172); Bridgeport at 6.30 p. m. (Carr); other regiments 3 to 6 miles beyond Taneytown on the road to Emmitsburg (R158 p530: "about midway between the two places").
- July 1: Emmitsburg 12 m. (Burling R174), 1.15 p. m. (Carr), about 2 p. m. (Austin), 2.30 p. m. (Baldwin); halted 1 mile out on the Waynesborough pike (R158 p530); order 3 p. m. (R158) or about 3.15 p. m. (Carr, after a two-hour halt); moved on "a road nearly parallel to the main road ... about 2 miles west of it" (R158 p531), crossed Marsh Run/Marsh Creek, took the left-hand road (R160 p543), then the Black Horse Tavern road (the road from Fairfield to Gettysburg), within 200 yards of the enemy pickets (Carr); counter-marched and came in by the main road; bivouac about 1 mile from Gettysburg east of the Emmitsburg road (R158 p531) at 1 a. m. (Humphreys, "la. m." Donovan), 1.30 (Carr), about 2 (Tripp, Bodine, Austin, Lockwood), 2.30 (Baldwin), "about midnight" (Brewster).
- Burling (3rd Brigade) and Smith's battery stayed at Emmitsburg on the Waynesborough pike / "Hagerstown road" (R158 p531, R174 p569); order 1.30 a. m., left nearly 4 a. m., joined 9 a. m. by the main Emmitsburg-Gettysburg road (R158 p531, R174 p570).

Artillery brigade: June 28 Middletown, Frederick (noon), Woodville; June 29 Taneytown (a mile north); June 30 afternoon Bridgeport; July 1 morning Emmitsburg; afternoon three batteries (Randolph's, Clark's, Seeley's) to Gettysburg, encamped on the left of the town near the Taneytown road (R181 p581). Smith's and Winslow's batteries came up on July 2 (Winslow left 3 a. m., reached the corps about 11 a. m., R183 p586-587; Smith with the 3rd Brigade at 9 a. m., R158 p531).

## 4. Conflicts between reports (all unresolved; linked in `conflicts_with`)

1. Corps, June 28 night: Woodville (R181 p581), near Woodsborough (R007 p144, date OCR "June 38"), Walkersville (R133 p482, R169 p558~, R172 p565), "7 miles from Frederick on the Woodsborough road" (R158 p530).
2. June 30, leaving Taneytown: 2 p. m. (Bodine R167 p556), 3 p. m. (Baldwin R161 p547, Tripp R162 p548~), 4.30 p. m. (Carr R160 p542), "morning" (Austin R172 p565); distance from Taneytown 3 miles (McAllister R164), 4 (Donovan, Baldwin, Tripp), 6 (Brewster R169, Hugo R170).
3. July 1, hour of the march order / start from Emmitsburg: "at 2 o'clock" Birney R133; 3.30 p. m. written order D0974 p465; Sickles to Howard "shall move" 3.15 p. m. D0972/D0973; Howard's message reached Sickles 3.30 p. m. R243 p703; Ward "arrived at Emmitsburg ... 3 p. m." R135 p493; 105th left 1.15 p. m. R139; 57th and 114th about 2 p. m. R136, R140; 2nd Division order 3 p. m. R158 p530, Carr 1.15 p. m. arrival then a two-hour halt R160, Burling at Emmitsburg 12 m. R174, Austin 2 p. m. R172, Baldwin 2.30 p. m. R161.
4. First Division at Gettysburg on July 1: 5.30 p. m. (Birney R133 p482); Hancock 5.25 p. m. dispatch "Third Corps not having yet reported" (R076 p366), head of the corps appeared on the Emmitsburg road shortly after 5-6 o'clock (R076 p369); about 7 p. m. (Meade R006 p115, Howard R243 p704, Bowen R140 p502); about dark (Ward R135 p493); a short time after sunset (Moore R148 p513). Ledger: dusk about 20:00.
5. Third Brigade rejoining: about 9 o'clock (Birney R133 p482), about 10 (R152 p519, R153 p522), about 12 m. (R154 p523); start from Emmitsburg 2 a. m. order, daybreak (R152), 3 a. m. (R154, R183), 4.30 a. m. (R153); Burling's order 1.30 a. m. (R174).
6. Second Division night arrival: see section 3 (1 a. m. to 2.30 a. m. and "about midnight"; ledger 00:00 vs 02:00); Hugo R170 p563 prints "arriving near Gettysburg at 12 m." for July 1.
7. Burling's brigade on the field July 2: 8 a. m. (Gilkyson R177 p577), 9 a. m. (Humphreys R158 p531, Burling R174 p570), about 10 a. m. (Woolsey R176 p575~). Burling at Emmitsburg 12 m. vs Carr 1.15 p. m. for the division.
8. Relief of Geary's division: 5 a. m. "relieved by the Third Army Corps" (Geary R293 p825) vs 7 a. m. (Birney R133 p482).
9. Berdan's reconnaissance: Berdan: first 100 men about 7.30 a. m., second detachment ordered about 11 a. m., reported about 2 p. m. (R149 p515-516~); Birney: 12 m. (R133 p482); Hunt: about 12 m. (R028 p234); ledger PO-23: 11:00, Pitzer's Woods (not named in the OR). Ward R135 sends both sharpshooter regiments and the 3rd Maine; Berdan sends 100 men of the 1st Regiment and the 3rd Maine while the 2nd stayed with the brigade.
10. Advance of the corps on July 2: notice "between 1 and 2 p. m." (Randolph R181 p581-582), about 2 p. m. (Hunt R028 p234), about 3 p. m. (Hancock R076 p369; Meade R006 p116, in the act of advancing), Birney: no clock time for the change of front, fire opened "say at 3.30 p. m.", the enemy at 4 (R133 p482); 4 o'clock (Gibbon R098 p416, Brewster R169 p558~); about 4 p. m. (Humphreys R158 p532); 4.08 p. m. (Carr R160 p543); about 4.30 (Hall R109 p436); Seeley engaged about 3 p. m. (R181 p584, R186 p590); attack opened 3 p. m. (Sykes R187 p592) or 5.30 p. m. (Norton R017 p202).
11. Graham and Ward on the line: Ward left and Graham right, in the Peach Orchard, with his right on the Emmitsburg road (Birney R133 p482-483); de Trobriand R152 p519: Graham's brigade "the left end of our front line", Ward en potence.
12. July 3, move of the First and Second Divisions to the center: 10 a. m. (Danks R137 p498), about 11 a. m. (Taylor R142 p506), about noon (Lakeman R143 p508), 12.30 p. m. (Baldwin R161), about 1 (Moore R148, Merrill R153), about 2 (Libby R145, Gilkyson R177, Austin R172), about 3 (Craig R139, Nelson R136, Bowen R140, Pierce R154, Pulford R155, Tripp, Donovan, McAllister/Schoonover, Hugo, Brewster), 3.22 p. m. (Carr R160), 4 (Higgins R146), about 4.30 (Humphreys R158 p536), about 5 (Cooper R178).
13. July 3 sharpshooters: Berdan has Capt. Baker's detachment covering the front of the Sixth Corps (R149); Trepp: with the Fifth Army Corps, Companies C, I and K, protecting batteries, the rest toward the First Corps (R150 p517); Stoughton: a dozen volunteers in front of the First Corps breastworks (R151 p519). Prisoners July 2: Berdan 22 (R149), Stoughton 23 (R151).
14. Leaving Gettysburg: July 5 (Madill R141 p506; Tripp R162 prints "the morning of the 5th", probably OCR), 4 a. m. of the 6th (Bowen R140 p503, Higgins R146 p512), orders on July 6 to move to Emmitsburg then halted (D1157, D1159, D1162, D1165; Burling "Monday ... a very short march" R174 p571); Sykes 1 p. m. July 6: First and Third Corps passing his camp (D1168 p560) vs Warren 2.30 p. m.: "General Birney has not moved from this place" (D1171 p562); 3 a. m. July 7 (Birney D1224 p586, Humphreys R158 p536, Carr R160 3.30 a. m.).
15. Artillery arrival: Smith arrived with the 3rd Brigade at 9 a. m. (Humphreys R158 p531); Winslow reached the corps about 11 a. m. (R183 p587); Randolph R181 p581: Smith and Winslow parked near Seeley's on arrival, placed after the 1-2 p. m. decision. Ledger: Smith at Devil's Den 09:00 (PO-28) and four guns above Devil's Den 14:00 (PO-27); Smith's own report fires first at about 2.30 p. m. "near the Devil's Cave" (R184 p588).
16. 73rd NY to Graham: Humphreys R158 p533 (after Sewell relieved the pickets, before Sickles was wounded, no hour) vs Brewster R169 (about 5.30 o'clock).
17. 2nd NH and 7th NJ ordered to Graham: Bailey R175 p573 "at 3 p. m."; Cooper R178 p578 "about 4 o'clock"; Burling R174: Birney's order.
18. Organization list R009 p162-169: footnote "Guarding corps trains, and not engaged in the battle" printed after the 84th Pa; in the OCR an asterisk also follows "26th Pennsylvania, Maj. Robert L. Bodine", though Bodine (R167 p555-556) reports the 26th in the line on July 2-3. Possibly a stray asterisk; not resolved.
19. Order to Graham D0972 (p464, "In the Field, near Getty ahurg" July 1) to go to Emmitsburg and command the troops there: no brigade or regimental report puts Graham there.

## 5. Differences from arrivals.json and placement.json (never resolved; Q04 not contradicted silently)

- arrivals.json us-3c-1d (first on field dusk, about 20:00, Cemetery Ridge via the Emmitsburg Road; also AP-02/PO-04): the OR puts the leading brigades at Gettysburg earlier: 5.30 p. m. (Birney R133). Moore R148 confirms the Emmitsburg road. Gate Q04 (never before the earliest value) would be contradicted by Birney's 5.30 p. m.; Ward ("about dark"), Moore (after sunset), Bowen (7 p. m.), Meade and Howard (about 7 p. m.) are consistent with or earlier than 20:00. Ledger place "Cemetery Ridge" is not in the regimental reports (they bivouac south of the town / on the left near the Taneytown road).
- arrivals.json us-3c-2d (00:00 EPP vs 02:00 SS1; counter-march at about 21:00 on the Black Horse Tavern road; AP-07 Black Horse Tavern Road): OR hours for the night bivouac are 1 a. m. to 2.30 a. m. (and "about midnight" Brewster); no OR passage prints 21:00 or any clock hour for the counter-march (gap claim). The road named in the OR is "the Black Horse Tavern, on the road from Fairfield to Gettysburg" (R158 p531, R160 p543).
- arrivals.json us-3c-art / subunit us-3c-art-4ny (Smith at Devil's Den 09:00): OR: Smith's battery joined with the 3rd Brigade at 9 a. m. but at the main road, parked near Seeley's (R181) and posted on the rocky hill after 1-2 p. m.; "Devil's Cave" in R184. The other four batteries have documented July 1-2 places (Taneytown road, Emmitsburg road, wheat-field), so "others gap" in the ledger is covered by this scope.
- placement.json PO-05 (corps advance 12:00-14:00 to the Emmitsburg Road): see conflict 10: no OR passage puts the advance earlier than the 1-2 p. m. notice (Randolph); most put it 3-4.30 p. m.
- PO-23 (Berdan to Pitzer's Woods 11:00): Pitzer's Woods is not named in the OR; hours 7.30 a. m. / 11 a. m. / 12 m.; place "beyond the Emmitsburg road", "the woods on the Fairfield road", "the Millerstown road" from the Peach Orchard (R133, R150).
- PO-24/PO-25 (1st Division 16:30 Devil's Den, 17:30-18:00 Peach Orchard): Ward's brigade is on the left from the morning (rocky eminence near Round Top / Sugar Loaf) and engaged from about 4 p. m. (R142, R147, R148, R146 4.30); Graham and the 3rd Maine are in the Peach Orchard from before 4 p. m. (R143, R138) until nearly dusk (R133 p483). Devil's Den is not named in the Ward reports; "Devil's Cave" appears in R184.
- PO-26 (2nd Division night July 2, Emmitsburg Road, 20:04 convention): OR: the division formed on the left of Hancock's corps along the Round Top ridge for the night (R158 p535) and returned to the Emmitsburg road line only as a result of the attack; by dusk it was off the Emmitsburg road.
- AC-03 (2nd Division advance 400-500 yards at 12:00): in the OR it is Birney who advances his left 500 yards (R133); the Second Division advances 300 yards (Carr, 4.08 p. m.) or "so that the first line ran along the Emmitsburg road" (Humphreys, about 4 p. m.).

## 6. Index gaps and absent reports

- Sickles: no report by Sickles in OR27 vol. 27 as indexed. The index has no record with that author (author search and full-text search of Part 1 for his signature gave only mentions and the casualty returns). His own signature appears only on Part 3 dispatches D0935 (June 30), D0973 and D0984 (July 1). Recorded as the gap claim on us-3c.
- Warren (chief of engineers, Round Top): no report in the index; Warren's June-July dispatches in Part 3 and the 1864 Mackenzie statement in the Meade composite were used where they place a Third Corps unit.
- No own report indexed for the 8th NJ (Ramsey), 115th Pa (Dunne), 12th NH (Langley), 73rd NY (Burns), 74th NY (Holt), 71st NY beyond a short note (R171) - see gap claims.
- Graham (wounded and captured): no brigade report; brigade-level claims come from Birney, regiments and others.
- R159 (Prince) covers only July 9 onward; R134 (French), R179 (Elliott), R180 (B. F. Smith) concern the Harper's Ferry troops (Third Division), which were not at Gettysburg. French's R134 gives the burial details of July 4-5 (tables unreadable).
- R130 "Aaron P. Seeley" is a captain of the 111th NY, not Battery K, 4th US (Seeley/James are in R186).
- Casualty and strength tables (R008, R013, regimental tabular statements) are unreadable in the OCR; strengths in the claims come only from the narrative sentences (Ward not 1,500; Brewster 1,837; Humphreys 5,000; Madill 200; Lakeman 14 officers and 196 rifles; Bailey 24 officers and 330 rifles; Cooper, Danks etc.).
- Interpolated pages (range printed, not a point): R133 (maps), R134, R136, R143-R145, R149-R150, R152, R154-R157, R160-R162, R169, R174, R176, R336, R009, D2101. Check the range before quoting a point page.
- Part 2 (Confederate reports): searched for Sickles, Birney, Humphreys, Trobriand, Burling, Excelsior, Berdan; no Confederate report in the index names a Third Corps unit with a time (hits are Pennsylvania militia officers named Sickles and an unrelated Excelsior mention). Hood, McLaws, Law, Barksdale are not indexed. No Part 2 claim was written.
- Meade composite R006: the "Historicus" letter and its comments (pp. 129-133) are a partisan newspaper account; not used as a source of Third Corps movements. The Mackenzie statement (p. 138) and the council minutes (p. 74) are used (flagged as after-the-fact / minutes).

## 7. OCR problems met in this scope

Dates and times: "June 38/39" for June 28/29 (R133, R160, R161, R007), "Jtdy / Jidy / Jvly / Juiy / Jyly" for July, "1^ miles" for 1.5, "1,15 p. m." for 1.15, "7. 30 a. rii.", "7.80 p. m." for 7.30 (R331), "la. m." for 1 a. m. (R163), "About 13 m." for 12 m. (R146), "By about 13 o'clock" (R030), "6. 30", "3. 30 p. m.", "1. 30 p. m." with spaces, "12_^m." in R174, Buford's date word garbled (R337 p928).
Words: "Emmitsbuxg, Aoout" (R136), "Sugar Xoaf" (R135), "Oh the morning of the 2d" (R139), "carap" (R146), "abeut" (R156), "imtil" (R162), "Walkers ville", "Taney town", "Sjkes" (R133), "Ernmitsburg" (R158), "Bimey", "Kandolph", "rqad", "Piizer's".
Garbled passages: R158 p532 (Third Brigade sent to the rear of the right of Birney's division), R169 p559 (74th NY "right of the First Bri- ^ gade"), R176 p577 (a footnote splits "Emmitsburg *Omitted. road"), R152 p521 (running head inside the sentence), R149 p515 (page split inside "divis- ion"). Quotes are verbatim from the OCR; OCR errors are not corrected.
Editorial brackets: R076 p366 prints the date and hour of Hancock's dispatch in a bracket ("5.25 [p. M., July 1, 1863,]").

## 8. Searches done (for the gap records and the audit)

Read in full: R133-R186 (all 54 reports of the Third Corps section; Sickles, Warren absent), R158, R160, R169, R174, R181. Part 1 searches for Sickles, Birney, Humphreys, Third Corps, De Trobriand, Burling, Brewster, Excelsior, Randolph, Graham, Peach Orchard over R001-R132 and R187-R378: used R006 (Meade narrative and composite), R007 (Itinerary), R009 (organization), R017 (Norton), R028 (Hunt), R029-R030 (Doubleday, Newton), R076 (Hancock), R098 (Gibbon), R109 (Hall), R187 (Sykes), R243 (Howard), R293 (Geary), R334 (Ames), R318 and R331 (McGilvery, Dow), R336, R337, R339 (Pleasonton, Buford, Devin). Part 3: all documents dated June 28 to July 7 mentioning Sickles, Birney (also "Bimey"), Humphreys, Third Corps, Excelsior, Trobriand, Burling, Randolph, Graham, Emmitsburg, Bridgeport, Black Horse, Marsh Run were read (D0840, D0889, D0895, D0919, D0921, D0923-D0936, D0960-D0984, D1009, D1093, D1099, D1105, D1107, D1110, D1116, D1154-D1171, D1224, D2101, D2102).

## Helper notes: notes_B_XII.md

# Notes: Scope B, XII Corps (Slocum; Williams / Ruger; Geary; Muhlenberg), positions ledger

Written by the XII Corps helper. Built by `cd tools/bt && python -I positions_claims.py B XII` from `tools/bt/positions_data_B_XII_a.py ... _i.py, _z.py` (helper `tools/bt/xii_helpers.py`) into `docs/battle-theatre/history/positions/partial_B_XII.jsonl`. Every quote is located and verified by the builder; nothing was typed from memory. Reports R271-R314 were read in full (R277 is unreadable, see 6). Part 1 was searched for "Twelfth (Army) Corps|Slocum" (193 hits outside R271-R314 examined), Part 3 for Slocum / Twelfth Corps / Geary / Ruger / Williams / Lockwood (291 hits; the 36 June 27 - July 5 documents were dumped and read), Part 2 for the XII Corps unit and commander names (320 hits: no Confederate report names a XII Corps unit with a time; Ewell R467 p. 446 quotes a Sykes-to-Slocum dispatch of 12 midnight).

Counts: 794 claims = 738 documented, 49 inferred (date or hour derived; reasoning stored), 7 open gap. By division (including their brigades and regiments): corps 64/5/1, First Division 320/23/4, Second Division 288/18/1, Artillery 66/3/1 (documented/inferred/gap). Dates and hours are as printed; approx=true where the OR says "about". The `conflicts_with` links are symmetric; a pair that prints the same clock time is not linked; a claim without a clock time is not linked to a timed claim of the same date (the time groups below show every printed value).

Strength tables in the XII Corps reports (R271-R314 and R013) are numerically unreadable in the OCR; only figures that appear in running text were taken (strength_note claims: Kane 652/650, Lockwood 1,700 / 2,500 / 2,100, regiment figures, Hunt gun counts).

Verdict reasons (corps, divisions, brigades, artillery brigade):

- **us-12c XII Corps: full.** Daily marches June 27/28 - July 5 with hours (Slocum R271, Williams R272, Itinerary R007, Part 3 orders), the July 1 move by Two Taverns, the Culp's Hill line July 2-3, the 7 p.m. departure, the night return, the 4-4.30 a.m. artillery, 10.30 a.m. end and the July 5 departure. Roads named: Hanover road (Littlestown), "Baltimore pike" (Williams) / "turnpike from Littlestown to Gettysburg" (Ruger).
- **us-12c-1d 1st Div Williams/Ruger: full.** Road (Gettysburg pike / Baltimore turnpike / cross-road to the Hanover road), July 1 hours (regiments), July 2 line (Culp's Hill, Rock Creek), 6 p.m. departure, return, July 3 fight, July 4 reconnaissance, July 5-6 departure. Dates of Williams's own report are doubtful (see conflicts).
- **us-12c-1d-mcdougall McDougall: full.** First Brigade: Littlestown June 30 (Hanover road), July 1 evening line 1.5 miles from the town and half a mile right of the pike, July 2 11 a.m. move by the Littlestown and Gettysburg turnpike across Rock Creek, 4-5 p.m. July 3 to the rear of the left center. Regiments 5th CT, 20th CT, 123rd NY, 145th NY, 46th PA each have their own reports; 3rd MD only via R278 (R277 unreadable).
- **us-12c-1d-colgrove Colgrove: full.** Third Brigade (Ruger, then Colgrove from the morning of July 2): July 1 turn off the pike about 2 miles from Gettysburg (R286), July 2 line along Rock Creek, 6 p.m. departure, 10 p.m. return, July 3 charge. Regiments each have reports (3rd WI R292; R291 Flood is June only).
- **us-12c-1d-lockwood Lockwood: partial.** Part 3 gives the march from Baltimore to the Monocacy (June 25-29). Between Monocacy Junction (June 29) and Gettysburg the OR gives no road or hour except the arrival at 8 a.m. (Ketcham July 2; Lockwood prints "July 3"). Positions July 2 evening and July 3 are documented for the regiments. Road of approach beyond "via Frederick City" is an open gap.
- **us-12c-2d 2nd Div Geary: full.** Geary R293 gives hour-by-hour marches June 28 - July 1 (Two Taverns 11 a.m.; advance 3 p.m. on "the turnpike leading from Baltimore"), the 5 p.m. line on the extreme left, 5 a.m. July 2 line, 7 p.m. departure, night, 3.30 a.m. attack, 10.30 a.m. end, July 5 1 p.m. departure.
- **us-12c-2d-candy Candy: full.** First Brigade: June 28 - July 5 with hours (R294), July 1 night on the left hills, July 2 line behind Greene, 7 p.m. to Rock Creek / pike, midnight return, July 3 reliefs, July 5 1 p.m.
- **us-12c-2d-kane Kane: full.** Second Brigade: June 29 4 a.m. (Cobham), July 1 night in reserve by the turnpike about 2 miles from Gettysburg, July 2 line (Kane/Cobham), evening fire at the works, July 3 3.30-10.30 a.m., July 5. Date of Kane's command differs (July 2 vs July 3).
- **us-12c-2d-greene Greene: full.** Third Brigade: July 1 night on the left (Taneytown road, Sugar Loaf), July 2 6 a.m. line on Culp's Hill, attack from about 6.40-7 p.m. to 9.30-10 p.m., July 3 4-10.30 a.m. No OR passage gives an hour or road for the July 1 arrival (open gap).
- **us-12c-art Artillery Muhlenberg: partial.** Muhlenberg R314 and Hunt R028: June 26 - July 5 with hours at 3.30/5 p.m. July 2, 1 a.m. and 4.30 a.m. July 3. July 1 night and July 4 batteries are given only as "about the same distance from the town" (1.5 miles); who Lt. Col. Best is (Slocum R271) is not stated. Open gap recorded.
- Regiments: every regiment and battery named in the brief has at least one claim. The 3rd Maryland is partial because its colonel's report (R277) is unreadable; the 3rd Wisconsin report R291 (Flood) is a June 6-16 report with no July claim (open gap recorded; July claims from R292 Hawley and R286). Brigades with NO claim: none. Brigades/divisions with an open gap for an item: First Division (Spangler's Spring), Lockwood (march roads), Greene (July 1 arrival hour/road), artillery brigade (Best, July 4), corps (Powers' Hill, Wolf Hill).

## 2. Approach roads and times (these decide where units enter the map)

**Corps (June 28 - July 1).** June 28: Knoxville, Md., to near Frederick, left 6 a.m., arrived 2 p.m. (Slocum R271 p. 758; Geary 6.30 a.m. / 2 p.m.; Candy 5 a.m. / about 3 p.m.; Robinson 5 a.m. / 2 p.m.). Orders of June 28 (Part 3 D0840): June 29 at 4 a.m. "by Ceresville, Walkersville, and Woodsborough, to Taneytown"; Slocum from Woodsborough 6.20 p.m. June 29: "not ... beyond Double Pipe Creek" (D0886). June 30: Taneytown/Bruceville to Littlestown (Itinerary R007; order D0895 "passing the Third Corps"); the corps encamped "about 1 mile beyond Littlestown, Pa., on the road leading from that place to Hanover" (Slocum R271 p. 758); Rogers: "on the Hanover road". July 1 (Slocum R271): morning to Two Taverns, then forward on news of the First and Eleventh Corps. Road: Williams (R272 p. 773) "the Baltimore pike toward Gettysburg"; Williams division report "Two Taverns, on the Gettysburg pike"; Ruger (R273 p. 777) "the turnpike from Littlestown to Gettysburg"; Geary (R293 p. 825) "the turnpike leading from Baltimore to that place"; Cobham (R303 p. 848) "the Baltimore turnpike to Two Taverns"; Muhlenberg (R314 p. 869) "the Baltimore pike to ... Two Taverns".

**First Division (Williams; Ruger from the morning of July 2).** July 1: from Littlestown on the pike to Two Taverns (2nd MA: marched 4 miles on the Gettysburg turnpike to Two Taverns; 5th CT there 11 a.m.; 20th CT "morning" then 1 p.m. forward), then at about 2 miles from Gettysburg left the turnpike to the right (Ruger R273; Colgrove R286: "about 2 miles of Gettysburg ... filed to the right, leaving the pike", then about 1.5 miles; Williams R272: "a cross-road to the Hanover road", to an eminence "a mile or so east of Gettysburg"; Grimes R289: left the pike about 3 miles out, then a road to the right). Arrival hours at the field: 5th CT 12 m., 20th CT 1 p.m., 107th NY about 4 p.m., 2nd MA "afternoon", McDougall "toward evening", 46th PA "evening". Night: withdrawn "about sunset" to a position 3/4 mile to the rear (Ruger), "about dusk" to the right of the pike (McDougall), "nearer the Baltimorepike" (Williams). July 2: Rodgers's 3.40 a.m. order to advance and connect with Wadsworth (D1002); the move to the Culp's Hill line is Williams 8 a.m., Slocum about 8, Ruger "about 9 a.m." by "the Baltimore turnpike toward Gettysburg" about 1.5 miles, McDougall about 11 a.m. "by the way of Littlestown and Gettysburg turnpike across Rock Creek".

**Second Division (Geary).** June 28 Knoxville 6.30 a.m. - Frederick 2 p.m.; June 29 4 a.m. via Ceresville, Walkersville, Woodsborough, camp 7 p.m. near Big Pipe Creek (21 miles); June 30 5 a.m. through Taneytown and Littlestown, camp at noon near Littlestown; July 1 left camp 5 a.m., Two Taverns 11 a.m., advanced "at about 3 p.m." on the turnpike from Baltimore (Geary R293 pp. 824-825); Barnum: Two Taverns about 12 m., left 3.30 p.m.; Rickards: left Littlestown 7 a.m.; Cobham: placed by Geary on a hill about 2 miles from Gettysburg (Second Brigade with two pieces of Battery K, reserve on the left of the turnpike). 5 p.m. line on the "extreme left", "half a mile west of the Baltimore turnpike to the left of the First Corps, to a range of hills south and west of the town" (Geary); Hancock R076 p. 368: "the high ground to the right of and near Round Top Mountain"; Greene R307: "in front of the Taneytown road, about half a mile to the right of Sugar Loaf Mountain". July 2 5 a.m. to the line east of the turnpike (Geary), Greene/Godard/Hammerstein about 6 a.m.

**Lockwood.** Baltimore June 25 (left that morning, D0746), Poplar Springs June 26 night, Monocacy June 27 night (150th NY, 1st PHB; Eastern Shore regiment a day behind), Monocacy Junction June 29 (D0884), "from Baltimore, via Frederick City" to Gettysburg, arrival 8 a.m. July 2 (Ketcham; "July 3" in Lockwood and Williams as printed). Road beyond Frederick not stated.

**Artillery.** July 1 "along the Baltimore pike to ... Two Taverns" (Muhlenberg); about noon the First Division batteries (F 4th U.S., M 1st N.Y.) away from the pike northeast toward the Hanover road and into position east of Gettysburg about 1.5 miles; the Second Division batteries (K 5th U.S., Knap) along the Baltimore pike, encamped the same distance from the town; July 2 the First Division batteries to the pike; 3.30/5 p.m. Knap/Van Reed on the right of the First Corps; then Slocum's Hill (SW of the pike) and the knoll east of it; 1 a.m. July 3 F and K SW of the pike.

### 3b. Other conflicts (value or composition differences)

- Lockwood's arrival date: Lockwood R282 p. 804 "8 a. m. July 3" (and the evening move "5 p. m. of that day"); Williams R272 p. 773 "Early on the morning of July 3"; Williams's letter in R271 p. 765-766 "early on the morning of July 2"; Maulsby R283 p. 805 "morning of the 2d"; Ketcham R285 p. 809 "about 8 a. m. July 2". The 2/3 digit is the dominant OCR confusion; claims keep the printed value with ocr_uncertain and the other date in the note.
- Geary's route on the evening of July 2: Slocum R271 p. 759 "took the road leading to Two Taverns, crossing Rock Creek"; Williams R272 p. 774 "the road toward Littlestown ... a position on the south side of Rock Creek"; Slocum/Williams letter R271 p. 766 "marching toward Littlestown by mistake"; Geary R293 p. 826: across Rock Creek to "the turnpike across Rock Creek Bridge", halted; Rickards R304 p. 851: forded the creek, 1.5 miles to the rear on the pike; Meade R006 p. 120: "mistaken the road". Hour of the order: Slocum no hour; Williams "Soon after Ruger's and Lockwood's commands had moved out"; Geary 7 p.m.
- Second Division position on the evening of July 1: Slocum R271 p. 759 (on Hancock's order) "on the extreme left of the line"; Williams R272 p. 773 "on the left of the First Corps"; Geary R293 p. 825 extreme left (hills south and west of the town); Hancock R076 p. 368 "the high ground to the right of and near Round Top Mountain"; Slocum's 5 p.m. dispatch D0979 "My Second Division has gone up to the town, and the First on the right of the town"; Greene R307 p. 855 in front of the Taneytown road.
- Greene's fight on July 2: duration "nearly three hours" (Slocum), "three hours' night conflict" (Williams), "two and a half hours" then discontinued about 10 p.m. (Geary), four charges 7 to 9.30 (Greene), 6.40 to 9.30 (Hammerstein), 6.45 to 9.30 (Barnum), "about two hours" (Godard).
- Kane's assumption of command: Kane R302 and Cobham R303 "6 a.m. on the morning of the 2d"; Geary R293 p. 832 "the morning of the 3d instant".
- Kane's return: Geary 9 p.m. (order), Greene about 10 p.m. (informed), Walker (111th PA) about 11 p.m. at Greene's rear, Creighton (7th OH) 11.30 p.m. ordered to form; Candy's brigade: Geary 1 a.m., Greene 1.30 a.m., Flynn 12.30 a.m., Stevens about 2 a.m., Candy "about 12 p.m." (midnight).
- 20th Connecticut / 123rd New York relief in the breastworks on July 3: Wooster R276 p. 794 after about 10.30 a.m. (immediately); McDougall R274 p. 784 and Rogers R279 p. 798 about 2 p.m.
- Time of the Colgrove charge (2nd MA, 27th IN): Morse R288 5.30 (a.m.), Fesler R287 between 5 and 6 a.m.; Ruger R273 after about 10 a.m. ("At this time I received orders"); Colgrove R286 no hour; Hawley R292 advance about 11 a.m.
- The reconnaissance around the right: Slocum R271, Ruger R273, Williams corps report R272, Packer, Rogers: July 4; Williams division report R272 p. 771 "July 5"; Fesler R287 p. 816 July 3 after about 4 p.m.
- Departure from Gettysburg: Slocum R271 p. 761 "The next day [July 5] ... Littlestown"; McDougall about 10.30 a.m. July 5; Price 10.30 a.m.; Geary and Candy 1 p.m.; Wooster "afternoon"; Robinson through Littlestown "about dark"; Packer's first report "11 a.m. ... July 6"; Williams's division report puts the first march (3 miles toward Bruceville) on July 6 with July 5 spent burying the dead; Slocum D1115 (Two Taverns, July 5, 6 p.m.) plans a 5 a.m. July 6 march to near Frederick.
- Strength: Third Wisconsin 246 (Fesler's footnote R287) against 21 officers and 339 enlisted (Hawley R292); 2nd Massachusetts 320 (Fesler) against 294 enlisted men (Cogswell R288); 107th New York 319 (table) against 322 enlisted (footnote) (Crane R290); Kane 652 (R302) against "only 650" (Geary); Lockwood about 1,700 (Geary, Greene) against 2,500 (Butterfield D0807) and 2,100 / 2,700 (D0673, D0693); gun counts July 3: Muhlenberg 20 guns, Geary 14 pieces, Price "some twelve guns", Packer "twenty pieces".
- The July 2 line relative to Rock Creek: Williams (R272 p. 773) "north of Rock Creek"; Slocum "across Rock Creek"; and Colgrove (R286) has the First Brigade on his right where Ruger (R273) puts the Third Brigade on the right of the First.
- Meade (R006 pp. 115-117, 120-121) credits Geary's division alone with the July 3 fight and calls Lockwood's brigade First Corps; Meade's own correction of Feb. 25, 1864, Slocum's letter (R271) and Williams (R271 pp. 765-768) are kept as separate claims.
- Division reports prefer different names for the July 3 morning hill: Ruger R273 "Gulp's Hill" [Culp's Hill] (OCR); Geary R293 "a continuation of Cemetery Hill"; Muhlenberg/Hunt "Slocum's Hill" / "hill known as Slocum's headquarters" (a different hill, near the pike).

## 4. Differences from arrivals.json and placement.json (OR claims recorded anyway)

1. us-12c first_on_field "Jul 1 evening on Culp's Hill: 123rd NY works begin Jul 1" (placement PO-39 / AC-11, 19:32 convention): the OR does not put the 123rd NY, or any First Division unit, on Culp's Hill or building works on July 1. Rogers R279 p. 798: lay on its arms near Rock Creek July 1; "Next morning ... moved toward the town, into the woods to the right of Cemetery Hill. Here the regiment ... built a strong breastwork" (July 2); McDougall R274: built breastworks after the 11 a.m. move July 2. Ruger R273: the Culp's Hill line was reached about 9 a.m. July 2.
2. "Williams's division approaches 17:30, withdraws to the Baltimore Pike for the night" (PO-40): no 17:30 in R271-R314. First Division regiments are near Gettysburg at 12 m. (Packer), 1 p.m. (Wooster), about 4 p.m. (Crane); withdrawal "about sunset" (Ruger), "about dusk" (McDougall), "At evening" (Wooster); "Baltimorepike" only in Williams's corps report R272 p. 773. Gate Q04 (us-12c earliest 1863-07-01T17:30) is later than the OR hours for the First Division (12 m. to 4 p.m.) and than Geary's 5 p.m. line; Slocum's own 3.35 p.m. dispatch (D0975) says he is "moving the Twelfth Corps ... about 1 mile to the right of Gettysburg". Reported, not changed.
3. "Geary on Cemetery Ridge 17:30" (PO-41): Geary R293 prints 5 p.m. and "extreme left of the line of battle", hills south and west of the town, half a mile west of the Baltimore turnpike; Hancock "near Round Top Mountain"; no OR passage says Cemetery Ridge for Geary on July 1.
4. "Lockwood's brigade arrives Jul 2 about 08:00 from Baltimore" (AP-03, road "Baltimore Pike"): 8 a.m. is printed by Ketcham (July 2) and Lockwood (July 3); "Baltimore Pike" as the road is NOT in the OR (only "from Baltimore, via Frederick City"); gap recorded.
5. night_positions "Jun 30: Littlestown / Two Taverns (123rd NY at Two Taverns)": the OR has the corps at Littlestown June 30 (Rogers: a mile outside Littlestown on the Hanover road); Two Taverns is the July 1 halt (Slocum, Williams, Packer 11 a.m., Wooster, Geary 11 a.m.).
6. "Jul 2: Culp's Hill works from about 06:00": 6 a.m. is the Second Division (Greene R307 "about G a. m.", Godard, Hammerstein; Kane/Cobham 6 a.m. command); the First Division arrives later (Williams 8 a.m., Ruger about 9, McDougall about 11; D1002 3.40 a.m. order). "Jul 2 06:00-10:00 works" (AC-07): works finished about 9 a.m. (Godard), about noon (Ireland), "By 13 o'clock" (Greene).
7. "18:30 departure of Geary, Kane, Ruger": Greene R307 6.30 p.m. for the First Division and Candy's and Kane's brigades; Ruger about 6 p.m.; Geary's order to his own division 7 p.m. (the First Division left "nearly half an hour previously"); Williams 5-6 p.m. orders. "Five brigades pulled to the left": the First Division brigades (McDougall, Colgrove) and Lockwood went to the left; Candy and Kane (Geary) did not reach it.
8. "23:00 Ruger at Spangler's Spring" (PO-36): no OR passage (Spangler's Spring is not named anywhere in OR vol. 27, parts 1-3); Ruger R273: after about 10 p.m. in line "along the crest of a slight ridge bordering the swale"; Williams: "on the open fields between the breastworks and the Baltimore pike". Open gap recorded.
9. "Jul 3 04:30 artillery opens" (AC-08): 4.30 is printed by Ruger, Muhlenberg, Hunt; Geary 3.30, Slocum 4, Candy 3.45, Packer 4, Price about 4; Williams "at daylight".
10. Positions for Powers' Hill (GZ-019) and Wolf Hill (GZ-024): not named in the OR; "Slocum's Hill" (Muhlenberg) / "hill known as Slocum's headquarters" (Hunt) carries no gazetteer id.

## 5. OCR problems met

- R277 (3rd Maryland, Sudsburg): upside-down scan, reversed gibberish; recorded as an open gap.
- Digit confusion that changes a date or hour: "July 3" for July 2 (Williams R272 p. 773, Lockwood R282 p. 804, Wooster R276 p. 793 "the morning of the 3d", Flynn R300 "morning of the 3d", Stegman R311 "July 3", Redington R309 heading "July 3"); "13.20 p. m." (Slocum D1040; 12.20 or 1.20), "13 m." for 12 m., "13 o'clock" (Greene R307), "G a. m." (6 a.m.) in Greene R307 and Pleasonton D1044, "lOp. m." (Geary R293), "15J o'clock" (Williams R272 p. 775, illegible), "6i.'36'a. m." (Candy R294), "about! p. m." (Barnum R313), "9 a. m." printed for night (Packer R275), "5. 55", "4. 30", "6. 30" with spaces, "4 a. TO." for 4 a. m. (D0840), "a. jn." (D1002).
- Name OCR: "Gulp's Hill" (R273), "LittlestoAvn", "Taney town", "Knoxyille", "Swell's corps", "T'aneytown road", "Kock Creek", "Eock Creek", "Vohmteers", "Penn- ^Ivania", "tte turnpike".
- Footnotes and running-head fragments inside sentences (R314 p. 869-870 "Divis- ... ion"; R287 p. 815-816).
- Tables unreadable: R274 p. 787-788 regiment strengths partly, R293 p. 833 table, R307 table, all casualty tables.

## 6. Index gaps and index notes

- OR27-1-R277 is delimited (p. 795, `ocr_inverted_page: true`) but unreadable. The printed report numbers differ from the index sequence in this range ("No. 283" for R282 Lockwood, "No. 383" for R283 Maulsby, "No. 380" for R280 Price, "No. 391" for R291 Flood, "No. 398" for R298 Hayes, "No. 293" for R292 Hawley); the index ids follow the sequence and were used.
- Page quality: R273 (Ruger) and R292 (Hawley) have `head-inferred` start pages; R276, R278, R282 and R298 show a doubled page head in the dump (harmless); 29 claims carry interpolated page ranges (R273 9, R271 8, R276 7, R009 2, R037, R211, D1008 one each); never exact.
- Sub-documents inside records are not indexed: the Williams-to-Slocum letter of Dec. 26, 1863 and Meade's reply of Feb. 25, 1864 (inside R271 pp. 763-770), Williams's letter to Geary of Aug. 8, 1863 (R272 p. 777), Candy's second report (R294 p. 838), Packer's two reports (R275), Ketcham's two reports (R285), Colgrove's two reports (R286), Maulsby's second letter (R283 p. 807), and in R006 the statement of the officer who carried sealed orders (p. 126), Gibbon's letter (p. 127) and an unattributed narrative (p. 129). Claims cite the containing record and say so in the note.
- Author strings with OCR errors in the index: "Euger" (Ruger), "McDougaU", "Robmson", "Wilham Hawley", "Bamum", "PoweU", "WiUiam R. Creighton"; report numbers and units are correct.
- Reports not in the index and not searched because absent: none for this slice (R271-R314 all present). Part 3 has no document-level contents, so the Part 3 claims come from dateline segmentation: D-numbers and printed pages as given by the index.

## 7. Open gaps recorded (searches tried are stored in the claim)

- POS-B-us-12c-1d-mcdougall-3md: R277 Sudsburg unreadable.
- POS-B-us-12c-1d-colgrove-3wi: R291 Flood is a June report only.
- POS-B-us-12c-2d-greene: no hour or road for the July 1 arrival.
- POS-B-us-12c-1d: Ruger at Spangler's Spring 23:00 July 2.
- POS-B-us-12c: Powers' Hill / Wolf Hill not named in the OR.
- POS-B-us-12c-1d-lockwood: roads and hours Monocacy Junction to Gettysburg.
- POS-B-us-12c-art: July 1 night / July 4 battery positions and the identity of "Best".

## 8. Statements about other scopes found while reading (for the lead and the other helpers)

- Fifth Corps (scope C): Slocum R271 p. 759 "The Fifth Corps arrived at 5 a. m. on July 2", placed on the right of the Twelfth Corps; Ruger R273 "the advance of the Fifth Army Corps" at daylight; Burbank R211 left on the right of the Twelfth; Sykes left Hanover 7 p.m. July 1, 9 miles to Bonaughtown (D1001, 12.30 a.m.), resume 4 a.m.; Fifth Corps massed near the bridge on Rock Creek on the Baltimore pike (Sykes R187).
- Third Corps (other helper): Geary R293 p. 825 "At 5 a. m. on the 2d, having been relieved by the Third Army Corps" (at the extreme left); Slocum and Sickles arrived about 7 p.m. (Meade R006 p. 115, Halleck R001, Howard R243).
- First Corps (scope A): Cutler R037 early July 2 between the First Brigade and Greene; Wadsworth sent the 6th Wisconsin, 14th Brooklyn and 147th New York (355 men) to Greene about 8 p.m. July 2 (Geary R293 p. 827, Greene R307 p. 856, Stegman R311: 6th WI about 8 p.m.); the 147th NY and 14th Brooklyn reported again to Geary about 8.35 a.m. July 3; the 147th PA and 102nd NY were sent to Wadsworth in the afternoon of July 3 (Pardee R301, Stegman R311); Robinson (R043) and Baxter (R052, 9 a.m.) in support of the Twelfth Corps July 3.
- Eleventh Corps (scope A): 82nd Illinois, 45th New York, 61st Ohio (about 400 men) to Greene (R293, R307); Dobke R256 and Bown R258 confirm; Howard R243 p. 704 about 4 p.m. sent Maj. Howard to Slocum asking one division to each side of Gettysburg; Slocum "had already ordered a division to the right".
- Sixth Corps (scope C): Shaler's brigade (65th, 67th NY, 23rd PA, 82nd PA, 122nd NY) reported to Geary 8.45 a.m. July 3 and was "posted as a reserve" (Geary R293 pp. 829-830, Slocum D1039 10.05 a.m.); Neill's brigade to the right under Slocum (R231, R223).
- Second Corps (lead): Hancock R076 p. 371 "General Meade brought up in person ... two regiments of Lockwood's brigade" on the left of the Second Corps July 2 evening; Caldwell R077 "relieved by the Twelfth Corps"; Gibbon R098 "timely arrival of the Twelfth Corps"; the California Regiment (71st PA, Webb's brigade, footnote R293 p. 826) reported to Greene about 8 p.m. and fell back.
- Artillery Reserve (scope C): Rigby's Battery A, 1st Maryland (R332 p. 899; R315 Tyler p. 873) fired at daylight July 3 "by direction of Major-General Slocum" across Rock Creek; Fitzhugh R330 two batteries on the Baltimore turnpike near Slocum's line; Osborn R264.
- Cavalry (scope A): Pleasonton D1044 (6 a.m. July 3) ordered Gregg to hold "the ridge on the Baltimore turnpike, behind the bridge, which is the right of General Slocum's position"; the 13th NJ, 107th NY and 5th CT went to support Gregg that evening (Ruger 5 p.m., Crane about 6, Packer 5/6, Grimes 7.30 p.m.).
